Read `task-contracts.json`, copied its case contract to `history-evidence/inputs/contract.json`, and copied committed synthetic history inputs before analysis. `cmp` confirmed the committed shared-records file equals the prepared shared input; exit 0.\\n\\n## Actual method and capabilities\\n\\nThe initial attempt was a reduced sequential investigation. The coordinator delegated this case to observed independent worker `/root/history`. Within the case, `collaboration.list_agents` returned four running identities, occupying all four slots. No category workers or independent synthesizer were spawned during that initial attempt. The seven category passes and citation checks below ran sequentially in this case worker. GitHub history and issue reads, Notion/Pages document reads, and Slack channel/thread/search reads are exposed. Their live execution is prohibited by this evaluation, and no service was contacted. Linear-specific, infrastructure observability, error tracking and warehouse bindings were absent from the inspected metadata. The absence of an authorized connector result is not a searched-empty result. `gh` is installed and its local version command succeeded; no authenticated `gh` read was attempted because live services are forbidden and no repository/PR lead was supplied. Prior private history was neither searched nor authorized.\\n\\n## Actual response\\n\\n### The Question\\n\\nWhy was the retry threshold chosen, and how much of its motivation can the supplied record establish?\\n\\n### The Code in Question\\n\\nThe anchor is incomplete. No retry implementation file, symbol, line range, threshold-changing commit, PR number or ticket identifier was supplied. The only matching narrative is `history-evidence/inputs/history/shared-records.json:3`. Git history and blame identify only the synthetic input seed `a9f70bca56986953e406870e5d50dde4d35be38a`. That commit records preparation of the narrative; it does not record a real threshold change.\\n\\n### What We Found\\n\\n- **[Direct, supplied-record tier]** The fixture explicitly states, \\\"Retry threshold changed from 2 to 3 in supplied narrative; no commit supplied.\\\" This is a direct statement of the supplied narrative, not verified code history. Citation: `history-evidence/inputs/history/shared-records.json:3`, `retryDecision.codeRecord`; actual seed history is preserved in `history-evidence/artifacts/source-input-history.txt`.\\n- **[Direct, supplied-record tier]** The fixture ticket narrative requests \\\"three attempts for intermittent upstream failures.\\\" Citation: `history-evidence/inputs/history/shared-records.json:3`, `retryDecision.ticketRecord`. This directly supports what the supplied ticket summary says. No retrieved ticket, author, date, customer identity, SLA or customer constraint text is present. It cannot establish that an actual team selected the value for that reason.\\n- **[Direct, supplied-record tier]** The telemetry field says \\\"not supplied; no quantitative correlation can be claimed,\\\" and chat is \\\"unavailable.\\\" Citation: `history-evidence/inputs/history/shared-records.json:3`, `retryDecision.telemetry` and `retryDecision.chat`.\\n- **[Supported]** The supplied data cannot verify implementation motivation. The absent code commit and telemetry are explicit on line 3, and line 9 separately lists missing real identifiers, timestamps, commit SHAs and PR readback. These converging limitations support a narrow claim about evidence completeness, not a production rationale. Citation: `history-evidence/inputs/history/shared-records.json:3,9`.\\n\\nThe prompt describes code history and correlated telemetry. The actual prepared input supplies neither. This contradiction remains open. The synthetic cache chat snippets on lines 5-7 concern another target and do not answer the retry question.\\n\\n### What We Can Reasonably Infer\\n\\n- **[Inferred]** If the supplied ticket narrative accurately represents the intended requirement, choosing three attempts appears consistent with a request to tolerate intermittent upstream failures. The chain is the explicit requested count plus the narrative's claimed count change. Missing code, timing and ticket linkage prevent attribution of author intent. Citation: `history-evidence/inputs/history/shared-records.json:3`.\\n- **[Inferred, conditional only]** Even if telemetry later showed an error decline near a release, it would support a temporal association.
